AT BRIRACT FROM "NIGHT SKY IN JANUARY"
Made to The Vest
Australian" newspaper by the PEKTH Observatory - 1 JAN 68
The nicht sky at the start of each calendar year is mede
particularly striking by the presence of a big number of bright stars.
Mara can be seen only in the early evening. Being nearly
three hours behind the sun, it will set about two hours after the fall
of derkness.
It is only moderately bright but can be identified by its
characteristic reddish colour.
Satur is slightly west of north and ebout 55 deg. above the
It is in the constellation Pisces and sets about mid-
horizon at sunset.
night.
change its position among the stars very little
during the next few weeks.
It is nearly in a line with
the eastern side of the so-called
great square of Pegasus.
above and to the east of them.
Jupiter rises about 10pm and will cross the meridian nearly
half-way between the horizon and overhead about 3am.
It is a very bright
object located in the constellation of Leo, only a few degrees to the east
of the bright star Regulus.
To the north-east of Jupiter, and rising only shortly before
midnight,
is the conetellation of Coma Berenices, which is an area not
obscured by interstellar matter and where many externel galaxies can be
found."

SIGHTING OF POSSIBLE UFO OVER IPSWICH
SUNDAY 19TH NOVENBER 1967
At approx 1930 hours on Sunday 19th November 1967 whilst
attending an outdoor social function at a friend's home I noticed
a bright object moving aeross the western sky.
After overcoming
the original disbelief I confirmed this and brought it to the
attention of 5 other people.
Those who were able to view the
object for any length of time were: LAC MeKENZIE, Mrs MeKENZIE,
my wife, a Mrs AHER who lives adjacent and her sister.
the opinion that this objeet was behaving differently from
I hed ever seen previously including satellites.
It seemed
still for short periods than move off again and was gradually
changing direction to due Worth.
It also lost altitude sharply whilst
changing courseyatill remaining very bright and once it had seemingly
adjusted it!s altitude gradually faded out in the Northern sky.
the many satellites I have seen pass over it did not "wobble" when at
it's farthest point of view, did not fade out as the sun's rays ceased
to reflect upon it,
and did not disappear over the curve of the earth's
surface on it's orbit.
To the contrary, it retained its brightness
and only faded out of sight due to the distance between it and us and
the linitations of human eyesight.
Whilst this object was in view
(at approx 1935 hs) another bright object appeared in the sky moving
from West to Bast and faded out abruptly overhead.
Most satellites I
have seen have faded out at a lower point of their trajectory than this
one did and did not abruptly vanish.
appeerance go, this looked very much like an ordinary satellite.
I vould like to state adamantly that these objects were not
shooting stars, meteorites, fireflies or any other well known phenomens
all of which I have seen many times during my lifetime.
Both objects
seemed to be controlled to set flight paths and the first object would
give the impression of being flown through a series of tests.
This
object would also be travelling so slow in comparison with a normal
satellite that I thought it best to report it for security reasons.

attended this Station and handed me the attached written report
of the sighting of an object described aa
a "ball of fire" which
appesred to tall to the ground between 7.20pm and 7.25pm on Monday

bob Colley of 13 Denison Street Mudgee and this youth reported
the incident to Mr.Graham, the Local Controller of the Civil
Defence Organization.
Master Colley was interviewed by me and the
contained in the report verified.

of Civil Aviation, Dubbo and spoke to Mr.D.Morris regarding the
sighting report. Mr.Morris informed me that the time of the sightin
was the approximate time of the crash of a R.A.A.F. Mirage Jet and
requested me to telephone the intormation to the R.A.A.F. Control

Dear Sir,
I an writing in connection with an
object Which I sighted last Saturday as I
have been unable to establish any
reasonable explanation of what the object
was.
The object was sighted from the road
between Kiandra and Adaminaby-three miles
beyond Kiandra, and was travelling in a
South Easterly
direction.Although first
impressions were that the object was a
Satellite, Comet, or Shooting Star, these
were discounted
as we (myself and
three
other witnesses) agreed the object was
travelling at quite a low altitude.

INVESTIGATING OFFICER'S REPORT ON UNIDENTIFIDD OBJECT
SIGHTED IN TRE SKY ON THE MORNING OF 23RD SEPTEMBER 1967
As the result of a UPO being sighted on 23rd September 1967,
Mr D. Sunderland of Maffra was interviewed on 25th September 1967.
The location from which the observation was made was the farm of a
Mr Welle of Riversles which is approximately 6 miles South West of
2.
From Mr Sunderland's statements, it would appear that the
object first appeared at 0530K moving from the North West to the South
Best for a duration of approximately 3 - 4 minutes.
Air Traffic Control, RAAF Bast Sale confirmed
that there were
no aircraft movements in the srea at the reported time of the sighting.
Further enquiries made to let Seotion RAAF Bast Sele, revealed
that the planet Venus was prominent in the area at approximately the
time as the reported UFO, and the reported movement of the UPO
coineided with the general behaviour of Venue.
The Investigating Officer concludes that Venus was the
contributory factor
the phenomena observed by Mr Sunderland.
27 Sep 67
(C.H.B. MILLERS)
Flying Officer

14 A
GORL
GOONAMBIL STATION

CORONA
August 13th, 1967
The Director
Aviation Meteorology Dept.
D.C.A-
Box 18399
Melbourne
3001
Met. Phenomena
Dear Sir,
I hesitate to write in case I am considered a "nut".
Perhaps your department may have some explanation for what I and
my three passengers observed when returning from Queensland on 9/8/67
in my Beech 23 VH-TKH.
We were on a heading of 185°M en route from Rockhampton
to Parkes. Area QNH was 1018, Altitude 6300°, Temp. + 12°C.
Vis 60 miles into haze, cloudless, nil
turbulence.
We had juat crossed the Queensland border at 2.22 E.S.T.
a mile west of TALWOOD.
A friend was flying in the R.H. Seat while
I was checking nav.
I looked up and ahead and saw what I thought
to be a large "mob" of birds about 2 miles ahead at about our altitude.
As my friend is not a pilot, I Indicated that I would take over to
take evasive action.
The "mob" was dense in the middle and strung
out in echelon to either side, dark grey
in colour and tended to
concertina in and out horizontally.
We all watched with interest
said they were ibie, from their foration.
I was surprised to note that the mob was travelling faster than we
were, was further away but still dead ahead and same relative height.
It continued to change its shape from time to time, either into a
round mass, or echelon right or left, and at one stage split in two
then rejoined.
At this time my wife noticed a similar mob bearing 45°
to starboard.
Both "mobs" in many ways resembled the erratic
flight of a swarm of bees.
For five minutes we followed
obviously moving away as they were
beconing less distinct.
The starboard
one suddenly disappesred, followed by the appearance
this time I remembered we had
field glasses, but they did not show much more than we had seen when
first sighted.
The images were more "cloud like" but in continuous
We followed the "mob" for 17 minutes until it finally
disappeared or merged into the haze horizon.
Had I been by myself I would have been worried, to say the
least.
As all four of us saw the same thing it was suggested that
I try and get an explanation.
I might mention that I have some 2000 hours flying exper-
lence, both in WW2 and during the last four years, and never have I
seen anything resembling the above, except that the colour was similar
to dark "flak" bursts.
Yours faithfully,
FRANK AUSTIN Unr.

cLoudy.

RESTRI CHED
NVESTIGATING OFFICERS REPORT ON UNUSUA
IGHTS OBSERVED IN THE SKY ON THE NIGHI
F 15th and 16th AUGUST 1967
Introduction
1.
On 17th August,
the Investigating Officer interviewed three witnesses
in connection with the sighting of
an unusual light in the western sky of south-
east VICTORIA.
The witnesses were:-
(a)
Robert MILNE - train fireman
(b)
Thomas
WILSON - train driver
(c) -
Robert HALDEN - police constable.
Witnesses (a) and (b) are the crew of a diesel-electric locomotive
which was travelling from YARRAM (VIC) towards MELBOURNE.
The initial
sighting, on 15th August, was made by witness (a), as the train was 1.5 miles
west of ALBERION 03838S
14640E, at 1902 hours (EST).
The light was visible
for 5 to 7 minutes.
The second sighting was made by the same two witnesses
the following night (16th August) at approximately 1910 hours at position
03842S 14606E between the towns of FOSTER and FISH CREEK.
The third witness
who was the police officer responsible for reporting the initial sighting, also
observed strange light on the night of the second sighting.
His observations
were from his home in TARRAM 03834S 14640E, during the period 1852 hours -
1909 hours.
Statements
3.
Witness (a) in his discussion with the Investigating Officer described
how at approximately 1902 hours (EST) on the night of the 15th August, he observed
a very bright light,
above the horizon on his left hand side.
The train was
travelling in a westerly direction.
The light appeared to descend rapidly, move
from left to right, move towards and away from him
then disappear.
The whole
sequence lasted approximately 6 minutes.
After observng the unusual movement
of the light, he drew the drivers attention to it with the phrase "Hey Tom, take
a look at this".
4.
Witness (b) in his discussion agreed with (a) on the times and location
of the sighting but was adament that the light appeared stationary.
5.
The following night (16th August) (a) and (b) agreed that the light
appeared stationary, in the same direction as that of the previous night and at
the same time.
On this occasion the light had a reddish tinge, as apposed to
the white light observed the previous night.
Also on the night of 16th, Constable HALDEN observed a strange light
from his back verandah in the town of TARRAM,
The light was first observed in
the western sky at 1852 hours.
It was agein observed at 1902 hours.
time it appeared lower and to
the left of its original position.
Under constant
observation it then appeared to move horizontally left till it disappeared behind
Direction of light source
From data supplied by Constable HALDEN, the Investigating Officer was
able to determine that the light source when first observed by him on 16th August
was bearing 275°T elevation approximately 5º and at its point of last sighting
272°T elevation approximately 2°.
Also on 16th August, the train crew observed the light approximately
20° right of the train heading.
From map reference 1t is concluded that in the
area of sighting the train line heade 260°T, consequently the light source would
be approximately 280°T, and alightly above the horizon.

•../2

===== PAGE 206 =====
{"alpha_words": 423, "chars": 2862, "method": "ocr", "page": 206, "variant": "plain-vision"}
RESTRICTED
-2-
9.
On the night of 15th August, witnesses (a) and (b) agreed that the
light wan finally sighted approximately 40° (angular figures supplied by
Investigating Officer from am movements by witnesses) to the right of the
train track.
At the last point of sighting the train had just traversed
a length of track bearing 250°T (Approximately 4 miles).
The light source
would therefore have originated from approximately 290°T from the area of the
train line, and above the horizon.
(Investigating Officer deducted elevation
of approximately 15°).
Explanation
10.
The data issued by Constable HALDEN was from a fixed point, and
measurable.
His sightings were 275°T 5°up, at 1852 hours to 272°T
at 1909 hours.
These figures caincide almost precicely with the movement
of the planet VENUS.
At 1900 hours VENUS was 274°T 4°up and at 1915 hours
271°T
1°up.

can also be corelated to

12.
Witness (a)'s reference to the light, moving from left to right,
could be attributed to a bend in the railway line.
In his statement he
said the light was first noticed 1.5 miles west of ALBERTON.
Reference to
maps shows that at 1 mile west of ALBERTSON the line changes direction from
280°T to 250°,
Consequently his initial sighting of VENUS would have been
very slightly left of track.
As the train negotiated the 30° turn left,
the
light would appear to have moved from left to right.
It is the Investigating
Officers opinion that this witness having witnessed the apparent light movement
then drew the drivers attention to the light.
ensured the safe negotiation of the bend, before directing his attention to
"l1ghts in the sky" - thus his argument that during his sightings the light
appeared to remain stationary.
On the night of the Investigating Officers interviews,
(17th August)
he personally made observations in the direction of the sightings and states
that VENUS was visible at the times stated.
His observations revealed that as VENUS descended, it dia develop
a distinct reddish tinge as it neared the horizon (confirming the train crew's
description), and did vary in brightness with atmospherie interference givine
an impression that it was approaching then retreating (as stated by witness (a).
Aircraft Movement
14.
RAAF EAST SALE had local flying only on the night of 15th and no
night flying on 16th August.
The 1ight observed could not have been an
aircraft from this base.
Meteorological

===== PAGE 207 =====
{"alpha_words": 277, "chars": 1803, "method": "ocr", "page": 207, "variant": "plain-vision"}
RESTRICTED
Heather
The sky on both nights was clear with a maximum of 3/8 at 5000 t.
The area was under the influence
of a large high
pressure system to the south,
There was, during the period a pronounced inversion
Maximum wind velocity on 15th August was BE/15 at 15000 It and
on 16th August E/15 at
is considered that weather would have
little if any optical interference or illusions.
Astronomy
17.
Last 1ight on 15th August was at 1800 hours.
On the same night
VENUS had a magnitude of - 3.8 This figure indicates an extreme brightness -
starwise).
Industrial
The direction of sightings does not corelate with any induetrial
complex, and passes approximately 50 miles south of
the city of MELBOURNE.
Comments
The fact that the sightings by the train crow were from different
locations
on each night, and
were then only approximate, renders the location
of the light source extremely difficult to define.
The facts and figures supplied by the police constable indicate that
his "light source" was the planet VENUS.
In view of the fact that all sightings were made between 1900 - 1915
hours, bearing approximately 270° - 285°, and slightly above the horizon, and
in view of the fact that in that area VENUS would have been clearly visible and
shining extremely brightly, and considering that despite VENUS's brightness and
the clear skies none of the witnesses made reference to it, the Investigating
Officer is of the opinion that the light reported was in fact the planet VENUS,
appearing unusually bright, its white light appearing red as it neared the
horizon and subjected to atmospheric interference.
Conclusion
The light source was the planet VENUS during its descent through the
last 15° to the horizon.
21 Aug 67
Bl. Sulla.
(B.C. SULLIVAN)
Flight Lieutenant
Investigating Officer
